Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_9x9` (precision INT9)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
- output ports: out[18]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os.fixed under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.03149 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 94 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 6.2834e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 395418, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.49857e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in norm: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt), then one LUT, into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 1.83 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.77 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in systolic_data_setup, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 5.07 ns in all, 2.12 of it into the last LUT, 5.988 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds four blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_9x9.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator out_temp — one block per seq_mac instance, each block's out is that instance's running sum (one product per sum, 256 separate sums)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.03149
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4666-4721): clk <- seq_mac's clk; reset <- seq_mac's reset port (driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, line 4110, through processing_element/_top_edge/_bottom_edge lines 4455/4551/4648); a <- {1'b0, a_flopped} (the existing 8-bit operand flop, line 4677/4684); b <- {1'b0, b_flopped} (line 4678/4685); out[15:0] -> the wire that replaces out_temp, read by the existing down-cast at lines 4707-4719; out[17:16] unused.

ASSUMES: whenever a PE's reset (effective_rst, line 4110) is sampled high at a clock edge, the product that was still in flight two edges earlier is zero, i.e. in_a or in_b of every PE was 0 during the cycle two cycles before that edge. Support in the design: the operands that enter the array are masked to 0 unless a matmul is actually streaming data — a0_data..a15_data and the b equivalents are AND-ed with a_data_valid/b_data_valid (lines 2632-2647), and a_data_valid is 0 whenever ~start_mat_mul because a_mem_access_counter is held at 0 (lines 2596-2606) and the valid test needs a_mem_access_counter >= MEM_ACCESS_LATENCY (line 2627); a0..a15, b0..b15 are those gated signals (lines 848-868). Interior PEs see only out_a/out_b of their neighbours, which the same reset clears (lines 4457-4466, 4553-4562). pe_reset is only set by an APB write of PWDATA[15] to REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR and auto-clears in IDLE (lines 4871, 4884-4887), and the reference testbench's own note states that write is issued between multiplies by protocol, i.e. with the array drained. A top-level reset aborts the matmul (start_tpu/enables cleared, lines 4820-4855), and output_logic holds c_data_out, c_data_available and start_capturing_c_data cleared while reset | ~start_mat_mul (lines 2304-2326), so no accumulator value from an aborted matmul is ever captured; the next matmul is preceded by the protocol pe_reset with the array drained, which re-synchronises the accumulators exactly.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted

Only one candidate is converted: qmult.$mul$...:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), all 256 instances. That product and the sum it feeds are the whole body of seq_mac (lines 4666-4721): a_flopped*b_flopped (qmult, line 4689), the pipeline register mul_out_temp_reg (line 4692), the 16-bit qadd (line 4697) and the accumulator out_temp with its synchronous clear (lines 4699-4704). That is exactly acc += a*b with a synchronous clear, which is what mac_int_9x9 is.

Edit, made once in seq_mac so that all 256 PE instances (processing_element, _top_edge, _bottom_edge, lines 4455/4551/4648) inherit it:

  • keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their unreset always block (lines 4677-4686) exactly as they are — they become the one extra register stage the block needs, and they must stay unreset so the operand sampled at the reset edge is still multiplied, as in the original;
  • delete mul_out_temp_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, the qadd instance and the out_temp always block; make out_temp a wire;
  • instantiate mac_int_9x9 mac_u1 (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0,a_flopped}), .b({1'b0,b_flopped}), .out(acc)); with wire [17:0] acc; and assign out_temp = acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];`
  • leave the down-cast (lines 4707-4719) and the module's port list untouched; leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file untouched (they simply lose their instances).

Correctness of the arithmetic: DWIDTH is 8 (line 74), both operands are unsigned wires and o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier (line 4728) is an unsigned 8x8 product, so zero-extending each operand to the 9-bit port reproduces it exactly, and the product never exceeds 16 bits. The design's sum qadd is 16 bits wide and wraps mod 2^16 (line 4738); the block wraps mod 2^18, and bits [15:0] of a mod-2^18 sum of the same terms are that mod-2^16 sum, so acc[15:0] is out_temp bit for bit. Nothing but the down-cast reads out_temp, and nothing outside seq_mac reads a_flopped, b_flopped or mul_out_temp_reg, so no value disappears inside a block (visibility is satisfied; out is the module's only output).

Correctness of the registers and reset: the block's reset is tied to the same signal that cleared out_temp, synchronous and active high, so out is 0 on exactly the cycles out_temp was 0 — including repeated and back-to-back reset pulses — and the block's registers are X only until that first clear, which happens in the testbench's 4-cycle start-up reset before comparison begins. The added path has no new design register: the pre-existing a_flopped/b_flopped serve as stage 1. The one behavioural difference is that the block also clears its operand registers, so the single product that the original still had in mul_out_temp_reg at a reset edge (added to out_temp one cycle after the clear) is dropped. That term is zero under the ASSUMES line above; it is the only thing the assumption covers.

  1. What is left, and why
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377 (sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380 through $413 (16 products, line 5070): the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits and written into variance_applied_data by a register with a three-way conditional enable — cleared on reset || ~enable_norm (line 5050), updated only while in_data_available || norm_in_progress (line 5059), cleared again otherwise (lines 5097-5104) — and the value must be held, not accumulated, while the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no load, its only control is a clear, and its output is a running sum that can never present a single product. It also puts the result 2 register stages after the operands where the design has 1. Not convertible.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530 (16 products, line 5482): same shape — data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal, a register with the same kind of multi-way conditional enable (lines 5466-5489), whose value is then added to data_intercept_delayed in the following stage. Again no enable on the block, accumulation instead of a held product, and one register stage instead of two. Not convertible.

These 32 products are also the only ones that touch the critical path, so leaving them is where the opportunity cost sits; see the delay section.

  1. Blocks and tiles

The rewrite instantiates 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, and removes 256 mult_9x9_fixed_pt multiplies. All 256 blocks are on the same clock (clk) and the same reset (effective_rst), so four of them may share a tile: 64 tiles for the array. The 32 multiplies left in norm and activation stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode; in the baseline the non-quadded products sit two to a tile, so budget 16 tiles for them. Estimated total 80 tiles against the baseline's 94, on a 132-tile device. The tile count is the smaller part of the gain: the real saving is soft logic, 32 flip-flops per PE (mul_out_temp_reg, 16, and out_temp, 16) plus one 16-bit carry-chain adder (qadd) per PE, i.e. 8192 flip-flops and 4096 adder cells leaving the clusters, against 0 added soft cells. That should take roughly a quarter to a third off the used logic-block area and a comparable share of the routed wirelength, at an unchanged clock period, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls well below the baseline's 1.49857e14.

  1. Delay

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: no block output feeds another block, each seq_mac is independent, and the sum stays inside its own block. The three stages the rewrite creates or changes, with the sampled numbers:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped (soft flip-flop) into the block's input register: 0.060 + wire register-to-first-block (1.314 to 2.253, median 1.940) + 0.019 setup = 1.39 to 2.33 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns, stated to be below the baseline's critical path.
  • the block's out register out through the unchanged down-cast (lines 4707-4719, two LUT levels) to output_logic's c_data_out capture register: 0.060 + wire block into soft logic (0.54 to 1.90) + two soft levels (0.32 to 2.91 each, median 1.14) + the wire into the capture flip-flop + 0.019, about 3.2 to 5.0 ns at the medians-to-worst. In the baseline this same path existed from the out_temp flip-flop through the same down-cast to the same register and was not among the two slowest stages; the only change is that its source is now a DSP output instead of a cluster flip-flop, and the array shrinking by 8192 flip-flops shortens the wires it runs on.

The stage that loses soft logic is the PE accumulate stage, mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp: the 16-bit adder and both flip-flop banks leave it entirely (it ceases to exist as a soft stage). That stage is not the slowest stage. The slowest stage is the one in norm (hard multiply, one LUT, flip-flop with synchronous reset, 6.03149 ns), and the rewrite does not touch it, so no speedup is expected and none is claimed: STAGE_NS stays at the baseline 6.03149 ns. The gain is entirely in area and wirelength. Note also that even a perfect rewrite of the norm multiply could only reach 5.988 ns, the next stage in systolic_data_setup, a 0.7% gain, which is why nothing is spent on those candidates.

  1. Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before: operands at seq_mac's a/b ports -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, line 4683) -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, line 4691) -> out_temp (stage 3, line 4699). Three register stages; a product reaches the accumulator in the third cycle, which is what NUM_CYCLES_IN_MAC = 3 (line 90) and row_latch_en (line 2276) are built around.

After: operands -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> the block's operand registers (stage 2) -> the block's out accumulator (stage 3). Three register stages, same 3-cycle latency, same value on out_temp every cycle. No register is added or removed on the design's visible timing; the design register that disappears (mul_out_temp_reg) is replaced by the block's own operand register one position earlier in the chain, which is why a_flopped/b_flopped must be kept rather than mapped onto the block's input register.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's clear at line 4700 also cleared a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg, or if the mul_out_temp_reg stage (lines 4681, 4691-4693) were dropped so the MAC had the block's own 2-cycle shape, the 256 PEs would map onto the block with no assumption at all: the clear semantics would match exactly, and the extra operand flops a_flopped/b_flopped (another 4096 flip-flops) could be absorbed into the blocks' input registers as well.

1	// mac_int_9x9: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^18 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[9], b[9] in; out[18] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_9x9 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [8:0] a,
17	    input [8:0] b,
18	    output reg [17:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [8:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [8:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [17:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 9x9 -> 18, exact
27	    wire [17:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^18
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

5037	
5038	//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
5039	//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
5040	//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
5041	//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
5042	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point

5062	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5064	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.

5075	            end
5076	        end

5079	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.

5082	        end

Call 2 · plan reviewer · APPROVE · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 38 · out 21312 · reasoning 16686 · cache read 1076938 · cache write 80087 · 300.1 s
prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 50 of the 94 tiles (the 256 element products among them; the split by family is not on file).

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator out_temp — one block per seq_mac instance, each block's out is that instance's running sum (one product per sum, 256 separate sums)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.03149
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4666-4721): clk <- seq_mac's clk; reset <- seq_mac's reset port (driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, line 4110, through processing_element/_top_edge/_bottom_edge lines 4455/4551/4648); a <- {1'b0, a_flopped} (the existing 8-bit operand flop, line 4677/4684); b <- {1'b0, b_flopped} (line 4678/4685); out[15:0] -> the wire that replaces out_temp, read by the existing down-cast at lines 4707-4719; out[17:16] unused.

ASSUMES: whenever a PE's reset (effective_rst, line 4110) is sampled high at a clock edge, the product that was still in flight two edges earlier is zero, i.e. in_a or in_b of every PE was 0 during the cycle two cycles before that edge. Support in the design: the operands that enter the array are masked to 0 unless a matmul is actually streaming data — a0_data..a15_data and the b equivalents are AND-ed with a_data_valid/b_data_valid (lines 2632-2647), and a_data_valid is 0 whenever ~start_mat_mul because a_mem_access_counter is held at 0 (lines 2596-2606) and the valid test needs a_mem_access_counter >= MEM_ACCESS_LATENCY (line 2627); a0..a15, b0..b15 are those gated signals (lines 848-868). Interior PEs see only out_a/out_b of their neighbours, which the same reset clears (lines 4457-4466, 4553-4562). pe_reset is only set by an APB write of PWDATA[15] to REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR and auto-clears in IDLE (lines 4871, 4884-4887), and the reference testbench's own note states that write is issued between multiplies by protocol, i.e. with the array drained. A top-level reset aborts the matmul (start_tpu/enables cleared, lines 4820-4855), and output_logic holds c_data_out, c_data_available and start_capturing_c_data cleared while reset | ~start_mat_mul (lines 2304-2326), so no accumulator value from an aborted matmul is ever captured; the next matmul is preceded by the protocol pe_reset with the array drained, which re-synchronises the accumulators exactly.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted

Only one candidate is converted: qmult.$mul$...:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), all 256 instances. That product and the sum it feeds are the whole body of seq_mac (lines 4666-4721): a_flopped*b_flopped (qmult, line 4689), the pipeline register mul_out_temp_reg (line 4692), the 16-bit qadd (line 4697) and the accumulator out_temp with its synchronous clear (lines 4699-4704). That is exactly acc += a*b with a synchronous clear, which is what mac_int_9x9 is.

Edit, made once in seq_mac so that all 256 PE instances (processing_element, _top_edge, _bottom_edge, lines 4455/4551/4648) inherit it:

  • keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their unreset always block (lines 4677-4686) exactly as they are — they become the one extra register stage the block needs, and they must stay unreset so the operand sampled at the reset edge is still multiplied, as in the original;
  • delete mul_out_temp_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, the qadd instance and the out_temp always block; make out_temp a wire;
  • instantiate mac_int_9x9 mac_u1 (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0,a_flopped}), .b({1'b0,b_flopped}), .out(acc)); with wire [17:0] acc; and assign out_temp = acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];`
  • leave the down-cast (lines 4707-4719) and the module's port list untouched; leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file untouched (they simply lose their instances).

Correctness of the arithmetic: DWIDTH is 8 (line 74), both operands are unsigned wires and o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier (line 4728) is an unsigned 8x8 product, so zero-extending each operand to the 9-bit port reproduces it exactly, and the product never exceeds 16 bits. The design's sum qadd is 16 bits wide and wraps mod 2^16 (line 4738); the block wraps mod 2^18, and bits [15:0] of a mod-2^18 sum of the same terms are that mod-2^16 sum, so acc[15:0] is out_temp bit for bit. Nothing but the down-cast reads out_temp, and nothing outside seq_mac reads a_flopped, b_flopped or mul_out_temp_reg, so no value disappears inside a block (visibility is satisfied; out is the module's only output).

Correctness of the registers and reset: the block's reset is tied to the same signal that cleared out_temp, synchronous and active high, so out is 0 on exactly the cycles out_temp was 0 — including repeated and back-to-back reset pulses — and the block's registers are X only until that first clear, which happens in the testbench's 4-cycle start-up reset before comparison begins. The added path has no new design register: the pre-existing a_flopped/b_flopped serve as stage 1. The one behavioural difference is that the block also clears its operand registers, so the single product that the original still had in mul_out_temp_reg at a reset edge (added to out_temp one cycle after the clear) is dropped. That term is zero under the ASSUMES line above; it is the only thing the assumption covers.

  1. What is left, and why
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377 (sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380 through $413 (16 products, line 5070): the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits and written into variance_applied_data by a register with a three-way conditional enable — cleared on reset || ~enable_norm (line 5050), updated only while in_data_available || norm_in_progress (line 5059), cleared again otherwise (lines 5097-5104) — and the value must be held, not accumulated, while the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no load, its only control is a clear, and its output is a running sum that can never present a single product. It also puts the result 2 register stages after the operands where the design has 1. Not convertible.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530 (16 products, line 5482): same shape — data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal, a register with the same kind of multi-way conditional enable (lines 5466-5489), whose value is then added to data_intercept_delayed in the following stage. Again no enable on the block, accumulation instead of a held product, and one register stage instead of two. Not convertible.

These 32 products are also the only ones that touch the critical path, so leaving them is where the opportunity cost sits; see the delay section.

  1. Blocks and tiles

The rewrite instantiates 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, and removes 256 mult_9x9_fixed_pt multiplies. All 256 blocks are on the same clock (clk) and the same reset (effective_rst), so four of them may share a tile: 64 tiles for the array. The 32 multiplies left in norm and activation stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode; in the baseline the non-quadded products sit two to a tile, so budget 16 tiles for them. Estimated total 80 tiles against the baseline's 94, on a 132-tile device. The tile count is the smaller part of the gain: the real saving is soft logic, 32 flip-flops per PE (mul_out_temp_reg, 16, and out_temp, 16) plus one 16-bit carry-chain adder (qadd) per PE, i.e. 8192 flip-flops and 4096 adder cells leaving the clusters, against 0 added soft cells. That should take roughly a quarter to a third off the used logic-block area and a comparable share of the routed wirelength, at an unchanged clock period, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls well below the baseline's 1.49857e14.

  1. Delay

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: no block output feeds another block, each seq_mac is independent, and the sum stays inside its own block. The three stages the rewrite creates or changes, with the sampled numbers:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped (soft flip-flop) into the block's input register: 0.060 + wire register-to-first-block (1.314 to 2.253, median 1.940) + 0.019 setup = 1.39 to 2.33 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns, stated to be below the baseline's critical path.
  • the block's out register out through the unchanged down-cast (lines 4707-4719, two LUT levels) to output_logic's c_data_out capture register: 0.060 + wire block into soft logic (0.54 to 1.90) + two soft levels (0.32 to 2.91 each, median 1.14) + the wire into the capture flip-flop + 0.019, about 3.2 to 5.0 ns at the medians-to-worst. In the baseline this same path existed from the out_temp flip-flop through the same down-cast to the same register and was not among the two slowest stages; the only change is that its source is now a DSP output instead of a cluster flip-flop, and the array shrinking by 8192 flip-flops shortens the wires it runs on.

The stage that loses soft logic is the PE accumulate stage, mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp: the 16-bit adder and both flip-flop banks leave it entirely (it ceases to exist as a soft stage). That stage is not the slowest stage. The slowest stage is the one in norm (hard multiply, one LUT, flip-flop with synchronous reset, 6.03149 ns), and the rewrite does not touch it, so no speedup is expected and none is claimed: STAGE_NS stays at the baseline 6.03149 ns. The gain is entirely in area and wirelength. Note also that even a perfect rewrite of the norm multiply could only reach 5.988 ns, the next stage in systolic_data_setup, a 0.7% gain, which is why nothing is spent on those candidates.

  1. Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before: operands at seq_mac's a/b ports -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, line 4683) -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, line 4691) -> out_temp (stage 3, line 4699). Three register stages; a product reaches the accumulator in the third cycle, which is what NUM_CYCLES_IN_MAC = 3 (line 90) and row_latch_en (line 2276) are built around.

After: operands -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> the block's operand registers (stage 2) -> the block's out accumulator (stage 3). Three register stages, same 3-cycle latency, same value on out_temp every cycle. No register is added or removed on the design's visible timing; the design register that disappears (mul_out_temp_reg) is replaced by the block's own operand register one position earlier in the chain, which is why a_flopped/b_flopped must be kept rather than mapped onto the block's input register.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's clear at line 4700 also cleared a_flopped, b_flopped and mul_out_temp_reg, or if the mul_out_temp_reg stage (lines 4681, 4691-4693) were dropped so the MAC had the block's own 2-cycle shape, the 256 PEs would map onto the block with no assumption at all: the clear semantics would match exactly, and the extra operand flops a_flopped/b_flopped (another 4096 flip-flops) could be absorbed into the blocks' input registers as well.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_9x9.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v

response

I'll read the design source and check the plan's claims.

APPROVE

Checks performed against tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:

  • Converted region. seq_mac (4666-4721) is exactly acc += a*b with a synchronous clear: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686, unreset), qmult (4689, unsigned 8x8 → ≤16 bits, 4728), mul_out_temp_reg (4691), qadd 16-bit wrap (4738), out_temp with if (reset) out_temp <= 0 (4699-4704). out_temp is read only by qadd and the down-cast (4707-4719); a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_temp_reg have no reader outside the module (only out leaves) — visibility holds. acc[15:0] equals the mod-2^16 sum since truncation commutes with the mod-2^18 accumulation.
  • Timing. Original term added at edge k = operands presented before edge k-2 (3 flop stages). With a_flopped/b_flopped kept in front of the block's own operand register, the block adds the same term at the same edge; 3 stages before, 3 after, as the plan states.
  • Reset. reset of each seq_mac is effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (4110) through all 256 PEs (4112/4129/4143 …, modules at 4455/4551/4648) — the same signal that cleared out_temp, synchronous active high. The one divergence the plan identifies is real and is the only one: the block also clears its operand registers, dropping the product the original still held in mul_out_temp_reg; from the second edge after the clear both match again.
  • ASSUMES. Supported: a_mem_access_counter/b_mem_access_counter held at 0 on reset || ~start_mat_mul (2596-2606, 3050-3060) with the valid test >= MEM_ACCESS_LATENCY (2627, 3081); operands AND-ed with the valid flags (2632-2647 and the b equivalents); the skew registers themselves cleared on reset || ~start_mat_mul (2771-2772); a_loc/b_loc tied to 0 (5887-5888) so a0..a15/b0..b15 (848-879) are those gated signals; PE pass-through out_a/out_b cleared by the same reset (4457-4466, 4553-4562, 4650-4659); pe_reset only from PWDATA[15] and auto-clearing in IDLE (4871, 4884-4887); reset clears state/start_mat_mul (5251-5255) and output_logic holds c_data_out/c_data_available/start_capturing_c_data cleared on reset | ~start_mat_mul (2304-2326). In the reference drive every reset edge (full_reset between runs, the 1-in-1024 pulses in the random phase, which never writes 0x04) lands with start_mat_mul low and the array drained, so the dropped term is 0; PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT is a non-default define. Power-up X is covered by the 4-cycle start-up reset before cmp is enabled.
  • Left regions. All 32 remaining candidates are accounted for: norm 5067-5076 and activation 5480-5489 both register a truncated single product under a multi-way conditional enable (5050/5059/5097, 5466/5477), which the block — no enable, no load, accumulating output — cannot reproduce; stage counts also differ. Valid reasons.
  • Required items all present: regions converted/left with reasons, 256 blocks and 80 tiles, longest block chain (1) with 2.219 ns against the 6.03149 ns slowest stage, the PE accumulate stage as the soft logic that leaves and that it is not the slowest, and before/after register stages.

One imprecision that does not change the verdict: the parenthetical "start_tpu/enables cleared, lines 4820-4855" is the cfg block's PRESETn branch (PRESETn is resetn, 5799), not top reset; the abort claim itself is carried by control at 5251-5255, and the testbench drives reset and resetn together.

1	// mac_int_9x9: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^18 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[9], b[9] in; out[18] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_9x9 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [8:0] a,
17	    input [8:0] b,
18	    output reg [17:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [8:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [8:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [17:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 9x9 -> 18, exact
27	    wire [17:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^18
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule
41

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
6	    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
7	    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.

13	    end endtask
14	    task apb_write; input [7:0] a; input [31:0] d; begin

19	    end endtask

24	        tick;

28	        tick; bram_we_a_ext = 0;

5038	//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
5039	//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
5040	//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
5041	//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
5042	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point

5052	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5053	        out_data_available_internal <= 0;
5054	        cycle_count <= 0;
5055	        done_norm_internal <= 0;
5056	        norm_in_progress <= 0;

5059	    end else if (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) begin
5060	        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
5061	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5062	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5064	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5065	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5066	        //this module in 1 clock.

5074	                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);

5076	        end
5077	
5078	        //Out data is available starting with the second clock cycle because 
5079	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.

5082	        end
5083	
5084	        //When we've normalized values N times, where N is the matmul

5086	        //that is taken in the beginning (when we are applying the mean to the first

5088	        //So, for a 4x4 matmul, this block takes 5 cycles.

5092	        end

5095	        end

5099	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5100	        out_data_available_internal <= 0;

prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int8). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator out_temp — one block per seq_mac instance, each block's out is that instance's running sum (one product per sum, 256 separate sums)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 80
STAGE_NS: 6.03149
MAPPING: per seq_mac instance (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4666-4721): clk <- seq_mac's clk; reset <- seq_mac's reset port (driven by effective_rst = reset | pe_reset, line 4110, through processing_element/_top_edge/_bottom_edge lines 4455/4551/4648); a <- {1'b0, a_flopped} (the existing 8-bit operand flop, line 4677/4684); b <- {1'b0, b_flopped} (line 4678/4685); out[15:0] -> the wire that replaces out_temp, read by the existing down-cast at lines 4707-4719; out[17:16] unused.

ASSUMES: whenever a PE's reset (effective_rst, line 4110) is sampled high at a clock edge, the product that was still in flight two edges earlier is zero, i.e. in_a or in_b of every PE was 0 during the cycle two cycles before that edge. Support in the design: the operands that enter the array are masked to 0 unless a matmul is actually streaming data — a0_data..a15_data and the b equivalents are AND-ed with a_data_valid/b_data_valid (lines 2632-2647), and a_data_valid is 0 whenever ~start_mat_mul because a_mem_access_counter is held at 0 (lines 2596-2606) and the valid test needs a_mem_access_counter >= MEM_ACCESS_LATENCY (line 2627); a0..a15, b0..b15 are those gated signals (lines 848-868). Interior PEs see only out_a/out_b of their neighbours, which the same reset clears (lines 4457-4466, 4553-4562). pe_reset is only set by an APB write of PWDATA[15] to REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR and auto-clears in IDLE (lines 4871, 4884-4887), and the reference testbench's own note states that write is issued between multiplies by protocol, i.e. with the array drained. A top-level reset aborts the matmul (start_tpu/enables cleared, lines 4820-4855), and output_logic holds c_data_out, c_data_available and start_capturing_c_data cleared while reset | ~start_mat_mul (lines 2304-2326), so no accumulator value from an aborted matmul is ever captured; the next matmul is preceded by the protocol pe_reset with the array drained, which re-synchronises the accumulators exactly.

PLAN:

  1. What is converted

Only one candidate is converted: qmult.$mul$...:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), all 256 instances. That product and the sum it feeds are the whole body of seq_mac (lines 4666-4721): a_flopped*b_flopped (qmult, line 4689), the pipeline register mul_out_temp_reg (line 4692), the 16-bit qadd (line 4697) and the accumulator out_temp with its synchronous clear (lines 4699-4704). That is exactly acc += a*b with a synchronous clear, which is what mac_int_9x9 is.

Edit, made once in seq_mac so that all 256 PE instances (processing_element, _top_edge, _bottom_edge, lines 4455/4551/4648) inherit it:

  • keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their unreset always block (lines 4677-4686) exactly as they are — they become the one extra register stage the block needs, and they must stay unreset so the operand sampled at the reset edge is still multiplied, as in the original;
  • delete mul_out_temp_reg and its always block, the qmult instance, the qadd instance and the out_temp always block; make out_temp a wire;
  • instantiate mac_int_9x9 mac_u1 (.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0,a_flopped}), .b({1'b0,b_flopped}), .out(acc)); with wire [17:0] acc; and assign out_temp = acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];`
  • leave the down-cast (lines 4707-4719) and the module's port list untouched; leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file untouched (they simply lose their instances).

Correctness of the arithmetic: DWIDTH is 8 (line 74), both operands are unsigned wires and o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier (line 4728) is an unsigned 8x8 product, so zero-extending each operand to the 9-bit port reproduces it exactly, and the product never exceeds 16 bits. The design's sum qadd is 16 bits wide and wraps mod 2^16 (line 4738); the block wraps mod 2^18, and bits [15:0] of a mod-2^18 sum of the same terms are that mod-2^16 sum, so acc[15:0] is out_temp bit for bit. Nothing but the down-cast reads out_temp, and nothing outside seq_mac reads a_flopped, b_flopped or mul_out_temp_reg, so no value disappears inside a block (visibility is satisfied; out is the module's only output).

Correctness of the registers and reset: the block's reset is tied to the same signal that cleared out_temp, synchronous and active high, so out is 0 on exactly the cycles out_temp was 0 — including repeated and back-to-back reset pulses — and the block's registers are X only until that first clear, which happens in the testbench's 4-cycle start-up reset before comparison begins. The added path has no new design register: the pre-existing a_flopped/b_flopped serve as stage 1. The one behavioural difference is that the block also clears its operand registers, so the single product that the original still had in mul_out_temp_reg at a reset edge (added to out_temp one cycle after the clear) is dropped. That term is zero under the ASSUMES line above; it is the only thing the assumption covers.

  1. What is left, and why
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377 (sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380 through $413 (16 products, line 5070): the product mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var is truncated to 8 bits and written into variance_applied_data by a register with a three-way conditional enable — cleared on reset || ~enable_norm (line 5050), updated only while in_data_available || norm_in_progress (line 5059), cleared again otherwise (lines 5097-5104) — and the value must be held, not accumulated, while the enable is low. The block has no clock enable and no load, its only control is a clear, and its output is a running sum that can never present a single product. It also puts the result 2 register stages after the operands where the design has 1. Not convertible.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530 (16 products, line 5482): same shape — data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] truncated to 8 bits into slope_applied_data_internal, a register with the same kind of multi-way conditional enable (lines 5466-5489), whose value is then added to data_intercept_delayed in the following stage. Again no enable on the block, accumulation instead of a held product, and one register stage instead of two. Not convertible.

These 32 products are also the only ones that touch the critical path, so leaving them is where the opportunity cost sits; see the delay section.

  1. Blocks and tiles

The rewrite instantiates 256 blocks, one per seq_mac, and removes 256 mult_9x9_fixed_pt multiplies. All 256 blocks are on the same clock (clk) and the same reset (effective_rst), so four of them may share a tile: 64 tiles for the array. The 32 multiplies left in norm and activation stay in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode; in the baseline the non-quadded products sit two to a tile, so budget 16 tiles for them. Estimated total 80 tiles against the baseline's 94, on a 132-tile device. The tile count is the smaller part of the gain: the real saving is soft logic, 32 flip-flops per PE (mul_out_temp_reg, 16, and out_temp, 16) plus one 16-bit carry-chain adder (qadd) per PE, i.e. 8192 flip-flops and 4096 adder cells leaving the clusters, against 0 added soft cells. That should take roughly a quarter to a third off the used logic-block area and a comparable share of the routed wirelength, at an unchanged clock period, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls well below the baseline's 1.49857e14.

  1. Delay

The longest series of blocks in any stage is one: no block output feeds another block, each seq_mac is independent, and the sum stays inside its own block. The three stages the rewrite creates or changes, with the sampled numbers:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped (soft flip-flop) into the block's input register: 0.060 + wire register-to-first-block (1.314 to 2.253, median 1.940) + 0.019 setup = 1.39 to 2.33 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns, stated to be below the baseline's critical path.
  • the block's out register out through the unchanged down-cast (lines 4707-4719, two LUT levels) to output_logic's c_data_out capture register: 0.060 + wire block into soft logic (0.54 to 1.90) + two soft levels (0.32 to 2.91 each, median 1.14) + the wire into the capture flip-flop + 0.019, about 3.2 to 5.0 ns at the medians-to-worst. In the baseline this same path existed from the out_temp flip-flop through the same down-cast to the same register and was not among the two slowest stages; the only change is that its source is now a DSP output instead of a cluster flip-flop, and the array shrinking by 8192 flip-flops shortens the wires it runs on.

The stage that loses soft logic is the PE accumulate stage, mul_out_temp_reg -> qadd -> out_temp: the 16-bit adder and both flip-flop banks leave it entirely (it ceases to exist as a soft stage). That stage is not the slowest stage. The slowest stage is the one in norm (hard multiply, one LUT, flip-flop with synchronous reset, 6.03149 ns), and the rewrite does not touch it, so no speedup is expected and none is claimed: STAGE_NS stays at the baseline 6.03149 ns. The gain is entirely in area and wirelength. Note also that even a perfect rewrite of the norm multiply could only reach 5.988 ns, the next stage in systolic_data_setup, a 0.7% gain, which is why nothing is spent on those candidates.

  1. Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before: operands at seq_mac's a/b ports -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, line 4683) -> mul_out_temp_reg (stage 2, line 4691) -> out_temp (stage 3, line 4699). Three register stages; a product reaches the accumulator in the third cycle, which is what NUM_CYCLES_IN_MAC = 3 (line 90) and row_latch_en (line 2276) are built around.

After: operands -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> the block's operand registers (stage 2) -> the block's out accumulator (stage 3). Three register stages, same 3-cycle latency, same value on out_temp every cycle. No register is added or removed on the design's visible timing; the design register that disappears (mul_out_temp_reg) is replaced by the block's own operand register one position earlier in the chain, which is why a_flopped/b_flopped must be kept rather than mapped onto the block's input register.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_9x9.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

Register stages are unchanged at three (a_flopped/b_flopped → block operand register → block accumulator), so the 3-cycle MAC latency the array's row_latch_en timing depends on is preserved. Both operands are unsigned 8-bit, so zero-extension to the 9-bit ports is exact; the block's mod-2^18 running sum agrees with the original's mod-2^16 qadd sum on bits [15:0]. This is the rewrite's only behavioural difference: the original still held one product in mul_out_temp_reg across a reset edge and added it one cycle later, whereas the block clears its operand registers too. I verified it at unit level against the original seq_mac with the reference mac_int_9x9 model: 0 mismatches over 20000 cycles with operands masked during idle gaps and resets issued only with the pipe drained, and mismatches do appear when resets are issued mid-stream — exactly the case the assumption excludes. The full tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v run is not reproducible locally (it needs the harness's $init_zero VPI task), so that end-to-end comparison has not been run here.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi

 "command": "cat /work/sim_reference/mac_int_9x9.v; echo ====; grep -n \"mac_int_9x9\" -A30 /work/blocks.vlib | head -60",

// mac_int_9x9: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^18 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[9], b[9] in; out[18] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_9x9 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [8:0] a,
    input [8:0] b,
    output reg [17:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [8:0] a_reg;
    reg [8:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [17:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 9x9 -> 18, exact
    wire [17:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^18

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule

module seq_mac(a, b, out, reset, clk);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
input reset;
input clk;
output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;

reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp;
wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_temp;
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_temp_reg;

always @(posedge clk) begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
end

//assign mul_out = a * b;
qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), .o_result(mul_out_temp));

always @(posedge clk) begin
    mul_out_temp_reg <= mul_out_temp;
end

//we just truncate the higher bits of the product
//assign add_out = mul_out + out;
qadd add_u1(.a(out_temp), .b(mul_out_temp_reg), .c(add_out));

always @(posedge clk) begin
    if (reset)
        out_temp <= 0;
    else
        out_temp <= add_out;
end

//down cast the result
assign out = 
    (out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
        (
           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
        )
        : //negative number
        (
           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
        );

endmodule

module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplicand;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplier;
output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] o_result;

assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;
//DW02_mult #(`DWIDTH,`DWIDTH) u_mult(.A(i_multiplicand), .B(i_multiplier), .TC(1'b1), .PRODUCT(o_result));

endmodule

module qadd(a,b,c);
input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] c;

assign c = a + b;
//DW01_add #(`DWIDTH) u_add(.A(a), .B(b), .CI(1'b0), .SUM(c), .CO());
endmodule

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.
