prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source before planning.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator out_temp — one block per seq_mac instance, each block's out is that instance's running sum (one product per sum, 256 separate sums)
BLOCKS: 256
TILES: 73
STAGE_NS: 5.47292
MAPPING: per PE instance (processing_element line 4375, processing_element_top_edge line 4472, processing_element_bottom_edge line 4568), for its seq_mac instance (lines 4455 / 4551 / 4648, module body 4666-4721): clk <- the PE's clk; reset <- the PE's reset port, i.e. effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110); a <- {1'b0, out_a} — the PE's existing 8-bit register of in_a (declared 4447/4543/4640, loaded 4463/4559/4656); b <- {1'b0, out_b[DWIDTH-1:0]} — the PE's existing register of in_b[7:0] (4464/4560/4657); out[15:0] -> the wire that replaces out_temp, read by the existing down-cast at lines 4707-4719; out`[17:16] unused.
PLAN:
This is the kept candidate's rewrite with one change: the block's operand register is fed from the PE's existing out_a/out_b registers instead of from seq_mac's private a_flopped/b_flopped, so those 16 flip-flops per PE (4096 across the 256-PE array) are deleted. Nothing else about the conversion changes: same 256 blocks, same reset, same tiles, same critical path.

Why this is the same arithmetic and the same latency. In the original, in_a/in_b reach out_temp through three register stages: a_flopped/b_flopped (4683-4686), mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693), out_temp (4699-4704). out_a/out_b are a second, already-present copy of the first of those stages: both sample in_a/in_b[7:0] on the same edge of the same clock (4463/4559/4656). Driving the block from out_a/out_b therefore keeps three stages — out_a/out_b (stage 1, soft, already in the design), the block's operand register (stage 2, replacing mul_out_temp_reg), the block's accumulator (stage 3, replacing out_temp and the 16-bit qadd at 4697) — so the result appears on the same cycle as before, and seq_mac's own operand flops are pure duplicates that can go. The two copies differ only in reset: a_flopped has none, out_a is cleared by effective_rst. Both this rewrite and the kept one already zero the term that the original would have added out of the unreset mul_out_temp_reg on the first edge after reset releases; see the ASSUMES line, which covers both.

Per-region decisions:

  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances: converted, one mac_int_9x9 per seq_mac. Register stages from operands to result, before: 3 (a_flopped/b_flopped -> mul_out_temp_reg -> out_temp). After: 3 (out_a/out_b -> block operand register -> block out). Soft logic leaving: the 16-bit qadd (4697), the 16-bit mul_out_temp_reg (32 FFs/PE including the product register width), the 16-bit out_temp register, and now the 16 operand FFs per PE. None of these stages is the design's slowest stage, so this is an area and wirelength win, not a delay win. What stays soft in seq_mac: the saturating down-cast at 4707-4719, which reads out_temp and so cannot go inside the block.
  • The 16 activation products activation.$mul$...5482$470 through $530: left. Their sum is slope_applied + data_intercept_delayed (5485), an arbitrary addend, not a running total; mac_int_9x9 offers only acc += a*b, so the add cannot move into the block, and extracting the bare product from an accumulating block needs a soft subtract of the previous accumulator value plus an extra register per lane — more soft logic and flip-flops than it removes. These multiplies already pack four to a tile, so no tile is saved either.
  • The 16 norm products norm.$mul$...5070$368, $371, $374, $377 (sum_in=top) and $380 through $413: left, for the same structural reason — each is a standalone product written straight into variance_applied_data (5070), with no accumulation for the block's out to carry, so each would need the accumulator-difference construction (a soft 8-bit subtract and a shadow register per lane, plus the muxing against the validity_mask path at 5073-5074) and would also require inv_var to be stable one cycle earlier than the original reads it. These 16 multiplies are in the design's slowest stage (the measured path: register -> multiply 2.140 -> one .names -> sync-reset flop, 5.47292 ns), and splitting that stage with the block's two registers would shorten it. But the next-slowest stage is systolic_data_setup, which at the baseline placement was 5.988 ns against a 6.03149 ns critical path — 0.7% below it — so the period can fall by only about that much once the norm stage is gone, while the added subtracts, shadow registers and nets raise area and wirelength by a comparable amount and these 32 norm/activation multiplies already occupy their 8 tiles four to a tile. Net effect on area x delay x wirelength is at best neutral and carries real equivalence risk, so this revision does not touch them. This is the deliberate departure from "shorten the slowest stage": the slowest stage is left alone and the gain is taken in area and wirelength.

Blocks and tiles: 256 blocks added, unchanged from the kept candidate. All 256 share one clock and one reset (effective_rst), so they pack four to a tile: 64 tiles for the array plus the 8 tiles holding the 32 untouched norm/activation multiplies, giving 73 as measured at seed 1 (down from the baseline's 94).

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block's out feeds another block; each out goes only into the soft down-cast and from there to the PE's consumers. The stages this rewrite creates are: out_a/out_b clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire (1.314-2.253, median 1.940) + block input setup 0.019 ~= 2.02 ns; inside the block 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns; block out clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire into soft logic ~0.96 + the two levels of down-cast logic + wire to the collecting register ~1.557 + setup 0.019, under 5 ns. All are well below the 5.47292 ns stage in norm that sets the period, which this rewrite does not touch, so STAGE_NS is that norm stage. The hop out_a -> next PE's in_a is unchanged except for one extra sink on out_a, which stays a register-to-register wire hop of about 2 ns.

Editor instructions:

  1. In seq_mac (4666-4721), keep the port list as it is, but note in a comment that a and b now arrive already registered. Delete reg [2*DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp, wire [DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out, wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] add_out, reg [DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped, reg [DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped and their always block (4683-4686), wire mul_out_temp, reg mul_out_temp_reg and its always block (4691-4693), the qmult mult_u1 instance (4689), the qadd add_u1 instance (4697) and the out_temp always block (4699-4704). Declare wire [2*DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp; and wire [17:0] acc;, instantiate mac_int_9x9 mac_u1(.clk(clk), .reset(reset), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}), .out(acc));, and assign out_temp = acc[2*DWIDTH-1:0];. Leave the down-cast at 4707-4719 untouched.
  2. At lines 4455, 4551 and 4648, change the instantiation to seq_mac u_mac(.a(out_a), .b(out_b[DWIDTH-1:0]), .out(out_mac), .reset(reset), .clk(clk));. The always blocks that load out_a/out_b` stay exactly as they are.
  3. Change nothing else. qmult and qadd become unused and may be left in the file.
    Widths: DWIDTH is 8 (line 74), both operands are unsigned 8-bit, so each is zero-extended to the 9-bit port; the product is at most 65025 and the original sum wrapped at 2^16 while the block wraps at 2^18, so acc[15:0] equals the original out_temp bit for bit.

ASSUMES: reset and pe_reset are only asserted with the systolic array quiet, so in_a and in_b into every PE are 0 at every clock edge that samples effective_rst high and at the edge before it. Support: effective_rst = reset | pe_reset (line 4110); the operands entering the array are gated to zero whenever no matmul is in flight, a0_data = a_data[...] & {DWIDTH{a_data_valid}} & {DWIDTH{validity_mask_a_rows[0]}} (line 2632 and the 15 lines after it) and b0_data likewise (line 3086 and after), and these feed a0..a15/b0..b15 (lines 848-851) and hence in_a/in_b; each PE forwards them with out_a <= in_a / out_b <= in_b (4463-4464, 4559-4560, 4656-4657), so the whole array is zero after a few quiet cycles. This matters because the original's mul_out_temp_reg (4691-4693) has no reset, so the original adds one product captured before the reset edge into out_temp on the first edge after reset releases, whereas the block clears its operand register; under the assumption that product is 0 and the two agree. The kept candidate already relies on the same assumption; this revision relies on it one cycle earlier as well (the operands sampled at the last reset edge), which the same gating covers.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if mul_out_temp_reg in seq_mac (4691-4693) were given the same synchronous clear as out_temp (4699-4704), the conversion would be exact with no assumption about the array being quiet at reset. Separately, if norm's pipeline (5067-5076) wrote the bare product of a stage into an accumulator that is cleared every cycle it is read — or if the product were allowed one more register stage — the 16 norm multiplies could also move onto the block and the design's slowest stage would be split by the block's registers.

Simulation

simulation passed: 4577 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows the outputs match on the cycles the testbench drives; it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_9x9.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the design source and the simulation artifacts.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan and the rules:

  • Plan regions only. The diff touches exactly the three seq_mac instantiations (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4455, :4551, :4648) and the seq_mac body (:4666–:4699). The PE out_a/out_b always blocks are untouched (:4457–:4466 etc.), the down-cast at :4685–:4697 is untouched, and qmult/qadd are left in the file unused as the plan allows. The only addition beyond the plan's literal text is the two-line comment at :4667–:4668, which instruction 1 asks for.
  • Ports. All four block inputs are connected at :4679: .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (= the PE's reset = effective_rst, :4110), .a({1'b0, a}), .b({1'b0, b}) — unsigned 8-bit operands zero-extended to 9 bits, as the contract requires. out drives acc, and only acc[15:0] feeds out_temp (:4682), which only the existing soft down-cast reads. acc[17:16] unused.
  • Same behavior / latency. Original: a_flopped/b_flopped → mul_out_temp_reg → out_temp = 3 stages from in_a. New: out_a/out_b (:4463) → block operand register → block accumulator = 3 stages. out_a <= in_a and a_flopped <= a(=in_a) sample the same value on the same edge, so operands applied before edge t land in the sum after edge t+2 in both. The block wraps at 2^18 and acc[15:0] is taken, which equals the original 16-bit out_temp bit for bit (max product 65025).
  • Reset. With reset high at edge R: original adds in_a(R-1)*in_b(R-1) at R+1 and in_a(R)*in_b(R) at R+2 (its mul_out_temp_reg has no clear); the block contributes 0 at both because it clears its operand register, and from R+3 on the two are identical with no assumption. That gap is exactly what the stated ASSUMES: covers, and its citations check out (:4110, :2632ff, :3086ff, :848ff, :4463/:4559/:4656). The reference testbench passes same-cycle on all 4 outputs across 4577 cycles with one mid-run reset; the pe_reset_in_flight diagnostic failure is the scenario the assumption excludes, and the recorded protocol is that pe_reset is never written in flight.
  • Visibility / terms / power-up. No deleted signal had a reader outside seq_mac; out_mac/out_c still come from soft logic outside the block. The single product term per accumulator is preserved. The added block registers are X until the first reset, and the run reports x_on_dut_only: 0.

1	// mac_int_9x9: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^18 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[9], b[9] in; out[18] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_9x9 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [8:0] a,
17	    input [8:0] b,
18	    output reg [17:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [8:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [8:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [17:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 9x9 -> 18, exact
27	    wire [17:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^18
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_9x9 (precision INT9)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[9], b[9]
  • output ports: out[18]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 18 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 18 bits (modulo 2^18), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified tpu_like.small.os.fixed under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.03149 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 94 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 6.2834e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 395418, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.49857e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in norm: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt), then one LUT, into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 1.83 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.77 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in systolic_data_setup, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; wire 5.07 ns in all, 2.12 of it into the last LUT, 5.988 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds four blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

Area is VPR's "Total used logic block area" in minimum-width transistor areas; wirelength is
VPR's total routed wirelength. On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_9x9_fixed_pt_mode packs four blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 2.3 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_9x9.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 6.03149
MAPPING: none — no further rewrite is proposed; the kept candidate (revision 1) stands, with its mapping unchanged (per PE: clk<-PE clk, reset<-effective_rst (line 4110), a<-{1'b0,out_a}, b<-{1'b0,out_b[7:0]}, out[15:0]->out_temp into the existing down-cast at 4707-4719).
PLAN:
No revision lowers the product further. Every candidate that the block can hold is already in a block in the kept revision, and the stage that now sets the period contains no block and no arithmetic this rewrite may touch.

Candidate-by-candidate.

  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances: converted in the kept revision (revision 1), one mac_int_9x9 per seq_mac (module body 4666-4721, instantiated at 4455 / 4551 / 4648). This is the one candidate whose sum is a true running sum with no enable: out_temp <= add_out every edge, reset its only control (4699-4704), so it matches the block's contract exactly. It stays converted; nothing in it can be improved further. Register stages, before: in_a/in_b -> a_flopped/b_flopped at edge k (4683-4686) -> mul_out_temp_reg at k+1 (4691-4693) -> out_temp at k+2 (4699-4704), three stages, product registered one stage before the sum. After: in_a/in_b -> the PE's existing forwarding registers out_a/out_b at edge k (4457-4466 / 4553-4562 / 4650-4659) -> the block's operand registers at k+1 -> the block's accumulator out at k+2, still three stages, the last two inside the block and costing no soft flip-flops. Soft logic removed from the PE stage: a_flopped, b_flopped (16 FF), mul_out_temp_reg (16 FF), out_temp (16 FF), the 16-bit qadd (4733-4739) and the qmult (4723-4731) per PE; what remains outside is only out_a/out_b (read by the neighbouring PEs, so they cannot go inside: visibility) and the saturating down-cast at 4707-4719 (reads out_temp, not expressible on the block's ports). That is the measured -22.9% area and -33.2% wirelength.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530, 16 instances: left. The product at 5482 is written into slope_applied_data_internal, a register whose update is conditional (reset || ~enable_activation at 5466, in_data_available || activation_in_progress at 5477, activation_type==1'b1 at 5481) and whose value is one product, not a running sum; the sum at 5485 adds data_intercept_delayed, a non-product term, one register stage later. The block has no enable and no load: it adds a term on every edge that does not clear it, so it cannot hold a single product while the design stalls, and it cannot add the intercept term. Clearing it every other cycle to make it emit a bare product halves throughput, which this per-cycle pipeline does not allow.
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) and $380 through $413, 16 instances: left, for the same reason. Line 5070 writes one product into variance_applied_data under validity_mask[i] (5068) with a pass-through alternative at 5074, inside an always block gated by reset || ~enable_norm and in_data_available || norm_in_progress (5050, 5059). Single conditional product, no running sum, no enable on the block. For the four whose sum sits in top, the adder chain is outside norm and reads the registered product, so the block could not hold that sum either.
  • All 32 of the above already sit on DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, so even a correct conversion would free no tile and no soft adder; the only effect would be extra blocks and extra latency-correcting logic.

Blocks and tiles: this plan adds 0 blocks beyond the kept revision, which instantiates 256 (64 tiles at four to a tile, sharing the single effective_rst and clk of line 4110 and 4112ff, measured packed into 95 tiles together with the 36 remaining soft-synthesised multiplies, against the baseline's 94).

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block's out reaches another block's a/b; each block feeds only the down-cast at 4707-4719. The block-internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, the stage into it is out_a/out_b clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire ~1.94 + setup 0.019 ~= 2.02 ns, and the stage out of it is 0.060 + ~0.96 + about two LUT levels ~= 3.3 ns. All are far below the measured 5.537 ns period, whose seed-1 path starts at a memory output and runs through one mux, three one-stage adders and two LUTs into $sdffe~3001^Q~3 with 4.77 ns of its 5.56 ns in wire. That stage holds no multiply, no accumulation and no candidate region, so no further movement of registers into blocks shortens it; shortening it would mean editing logic the rewrite does not need, which scope forbids. Nothing the rewrite leaves behind lengthened any other stage: the baseline's slowest stage (norm, hard multiply then one LUT, 6.03149 ns) is untouched and got faster only through placement.

Why no further revision: the only remaining soft logic in the converted region is the PE forwarding registers out_a/out_b and the per-PE down-cast, and neither can move inside a block — out_a and out_b are read by neighbouring PEs (4112-4143 and the rest of the array) and by nothing the block exposes, and the down-cast is not an arithmetic the block's ports carry. Feeding a block from in_a/in_b instead of out_a/out_b would place its result one cycle early and require 8 new soft flip-flops per PE to re-align, strictly worse on area than the current zero. Regrouping the 16 MACs of a row or column into one accumulating block is impossible: each PE's out_c is read separately (4453 / 4549 / 4646), so the 256 sums must stay 256 sums. Area, period and wirelength are therefore all at the floor of this rewrite family.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the norm and activation pipelines (5049-5105, 5465-5520) update their product registers under enables (enable_norm, in_data_available || norm_in_progress, validity_mask, enable_activation, activation_type); if those stages ran unconditionally with their inputs zeroed when idle, each product would become a single-term sum that an accumulating block cleared by the same reset could hold, putting 32 more products on blocks. Separately, bits [18:8] of the in_b/out_b chain (4443-4466 and the 19-bit b*to* wires at 4088-4103) feed nothing but further out_b registers and end unconnected (b_data_out is undriven at 4371 and tied to b_data_out_NC at 5877); narrowing that chain to 8 bits would make the dead-bit sweep explicit rather than relying on synthesis.

374	//This is 7 bits because the expectation is that clock count will be pretty
375	//small. For large matmuls, this will need to increased to have more bits.
376	//In general, a systolic multiplier takes 4*N-2+P cycles, where N is the size 
377	//of the matmul and P is the number of pipleine stages in the MAC block.
378	reg [7:0] clk_cnt;
379	
380	//Finding out number of cycles to assert matmul done.
381	//When we have to save the outputs to accumulators, then we don't need to
382	//shift out data. So, we can assert done_mat_mul early.
383	//In the normal case, we have to include the time to shift out the results. 
384	//Note: the count expression used to contain "4*final_mat_mul_size", but 
385	//to avoid multiplication, we now use "final_mat_mul_size<<2"
386	wire [7:0] clk_cnt_for_done;
387

389	                          ((`final_mat_mul_size<<2) - 2 + `NUM_CYCLES_IN_MAC);  
390	    
391	always @(posedge clk) begin
392	  if (reset || ~start_mat_mul) begin
393	    clk_cnt <= 0;
394	    done_mat_mul <= 0;
395	  end
396	  else if (clk_cnt == clk_cnt_for_done) begin
397	    done_mat_mul <= 1;
398	    clk_cnt <= clk_cnt + 1;
399	
400	  end
401	  else if (done_mat_mul == 0) begin
402	    clk_cnt <= clk_cnt + 1;
403	
404	  end    
405	  else begin
406	    done_mat_mul <= 0;
407	    clk_cnt <= clk_cnt + 1;
408	  end
409	end

5007	module norm(
5008	    input enable_norm,
5009	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
5010	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
5011	    input in_data_available,
5012	    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5013	    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5014	    output out_data_available,
5015	    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
5016	    output done_norm,
5017	    input clk,
5018	    input reset
5019	);
5020	
5021	reg out_data_available_internal;
5022	wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5023	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
5024	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
5025	reg done_norm_internal;
5026	reg norm_in_progress;
5027	reg in_data_available_flopped;
5028	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;
5029	
5030	//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
5031	assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;
5032	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
5033	assign done_norm = (enable_norm) ? done_norm_internal : 1'b1;
5034	
5035	//inp_data will have multiple elements in it. the number of elements is the same as size of the matmul.
5036	//on each clock edge, if in_data_available is 1, then we will normalize the inputs.
5037	
5038	//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
5039	//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
5040	//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
5041	//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
5042	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point
5043	//another example:
5044	//loc = 3;
5045	//PA[loc -:4] = PA[loc+1 +:4];  // equivalent to PA[3:0] = PA[7:4];
5046	
5047	reg [31:0] cycle_count;
5048	reg [31:0] i;
5049	always @(posedge clk) begin
5050	    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
5051	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5052	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5053	        out_data_available_internal <= 0;
5054	        cycle_count <= 0;
5055	        done_norm_internal <= 0;
5056	        norm_in_progress <= 0;
5057	        in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
5058	        inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
5059	    end else if (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) begin
5060	        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
5061	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5062	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5064	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.

1	// mac_int_9x9: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^18 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[9], b[9] in; out[18] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_9x9 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [8:0] a,
17	    input [8:0] b,
18	    output reg [17:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [8:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [8:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [17:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 9x9 -> 18, exact
27	    wire [17:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^18
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.2834e+07 · critical path 6.03149 ns · wirelength 395418 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 1.49857e+14 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.76%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.76%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

Pass 1 product 7.71184e+13 (-48.54%)
tier
T2
plan header
BLOCKS 256 · TILES 80 · STAGE_NS 6.03149
assumptions
whenever a PE's reset (`effective_rst`, line 4110) is sampled high at a clock edge, the product that was still in flight two edges earlier is zero, i.e. `in_a` or `in_b` of every PE was 0 during the cycle two cycles before that edge. Support in the design: the operands that enter the array are masked to 0 unless a matmul is actually streaming data — `a0_data`..`a15_data` and the b equivalents are AND-ed with `a_data_valid`/`b_data_valid` (lines 2632-2647), and `a_data_valid` is 0 whenever `~start_mat_mul` because `a_mem_access_counter` is held at 0 (lines 2596-2606) and the valid test needs `a_mem_access_counter >= MEM_ACCESS_LATENCY` (line 2627); `a0`..`a15`, `b0`..`b15` are those gated signals (lines 848-868). Interior PEs see only `out_a`/`out_b` of their neighbours, which the same reset clears (lines 4457-4466, 4553-4562). `pe_reset` is only set by an APB write of PWDATA[15] to REG_STDN_TPU_ADDR and auto-clears in IDLE (lines 4871, 4884-4887), and the reference testbench's own note states that write is issued between multiplies by protocol, i.e. with the array drained. A top-level `reset` aborts the matmul (`start_tpu`/enables cleared, lines 4820-4855), and `output_logic` holds `c_data_out`, `c_data_available` and `start_capturing_c_data` cleared while `reset | ~start_mat_mul` (lines 2304-2326), so no accumulator value from an aborted matmul is ever captured; the next matmul is preceded by the protocol `pe_reset` with the array drained, which re-synchronises the accumulators exactly.

Pass 2 · kept product 7.2544e+13 (-51.59%)
tier
T2
plan header
BLOCKS 256 · TILES 73 · STAGE_NS 5.47292
assumptions
reset and pe_reset are only asserted with the systolic array quiet, so `in_a` and `in_b` into every PE are 0 at every clock edge that samples `effective_rst` high and at the edge before it. Support: `effective_rst = reset | pe_reset` (line 4110); the operands entering the array are gated to zero whenever no matmul is in flight, `a0_data = a_data[...] & {DWIDTH{a_data_valid}} & {DWIDTH{validity_mask_a_rows[0]}}` (line 2632 and the 15 lines after it) and `b0_data` likewise (line 3086 and after), and these feed a0..a15/b0..b15 (lines 848-851) and hence `in_a`/`in_b`; each PE forwards them with `out_a <= in_a` / `out_b <= in_b` (4463-4464, 4559-4560, 4656-4657), so the whole array is zero after a few quiet cycles. This matters because the original's `mul_out_temp_reg` (4691-4693) has no reset, so the original adds one product captured before the reset edge into `out_temp` on the first edge after reset releases, whereas the block clears its operand register; under the assumption that product is 0 and the two agree. The kept candidate already relies on the same assumption; this revision relies on it one cycle earlier as well (the operands sampled at the last reset edge), which the same gating covers.

I'll read the design source before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 94
STAGE_NS: 6.03149
MAPPING: none — no further rewrite is proposed; the kept candidate (revision 1) stands, with its mapping unchanged (per PE: clk<-PE clk, reset<-effective_rst (line 4110), a<-{1'b0,out_a}, b<-{1'b0,out_b[7:0]}, out[15:0]->out_temp into the existing down-cast at 4707-4719).
PLAN:
No revision lowers the product further. Every candidate that the block can hold is already in a block in the kept revision, and the stage that now sets the period contains no block and no arithmetic this rewrite may touch.

Candidate-by-candidate.

  • qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances: converted in the kept revision (revision 1), one mac_int_9x9 per seq_mac (module body 4666-4721, instantiated at 4455 / 4551 / 4648). This is the one candidate whose sum is a true running sum with no enable: out_temp <= add_out every edge, reset its only control (4699-4704), so it matches the block's contract exactly. It stays converted; nothing in it can be improved further. Register stages, before: in_a/in_b -> a_flopped/b_flopped at edge k (4683-4686) -> mul_out_temp_reg at k+1 (4691-4693) -> out_temp at k+2 (4699-4704), three stages, product registered one stage before the sum. After: in_a/in_b -> the PE's existing forwarding registers out_a/out_b at edge k (4457-4466 / 4553-4562 / 4650-4659) -> the block's operand registers at k+1 -> the block's accumulator out at k+2, still three stages, the last two inside the block and costing no soft flip-flops. Soft logic removed from the PE stage: a_flopped, b_flopped (16 FF), mul_out_temp_reg (16 FF), out_temp (16 FF), the 16-bit qadd (4733-4739) and the qmult (4723-4731) per PE; what remains outside is only out_a/out_b (read by the neighbouring PEs, so they cannot go inside: visibility) and the saturating down-cast at 4707-4719 (reads out_temp, not expressible on the block's ports). That is the measured -22.9% area and -33.2% wirelength.
  • activation.$mul$...:5482$470 through $530, 16 instances: left. The product at 5482 is written into slope_applied_data_internal, a register whose update is conditional (reset || ~enable_activation at 5466, in_data_available || activation_in_progress at 5477, activation_type==1'b1 at 5481) and whose value is one product, not a running sum; the sum at 5485 adds data_intercept_delayed, a non-product term, one register stage later. The block has no enable and no load: it adds a term on every edge that does not clear it, so it cannot hold a single product while the design stalls, and it cannot add the intercept term. Clearing it every other cycle to make it emit a bare product halves throughput, which this per-cycle pipeline does not allow.
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) and $380 through $413, 16 instances: left, for the same reason. Line 5070 writes one product into variance_applied_data under validity_mask[i] (5068) with a pass-through alternative at 5074, inside an always block gated by reset || ~enable_norm and in_data_available || norm_in_progress (5050, 5059). Single conditional product, no running sum, no enable on the block. For the four whose sum sits in top, the adder chain is outside norm and reads the registered product, so the block could not hold that sum either.
  • All 32 of the above already sit on DSP tiles in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, so even a correct conversion would free no tile and no soft adder; the only effect would be extra blocks and extra latency-correcting logic.

Blocks and tiles: this plan adds 0 blocks beyond the kept revision, which instantiates 256 (64 tiles at four to a tile, sharing the single effective_rst and clk of line 4110 and 4112ff, measured packed into 95 tiles together with the 36 remaining soft-synthesised multiplies, against the baseline's 94).

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. No block's out reaches another block's a/b; each block feeds only the down-cast at 4707-4719. The block-internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, the stage into it is out_a/out_b clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire ~1.94 + setup 0.019 ~= 2.02 ns, and the stage out of it is 0.060 + ~0.96 + about two LUT levels ~= 3.3 ns. All are far below the measured 5.537 ns period, whose seed-1 path starts at a memory output and runs through one mux, three one-stage adders and two LUTs into $sdffe~3001^Q~3 with 4.77 ns of its 5.56 ns in wire. That stage holds no multiply, no accumulation and no candidate region, so no further movement of registers into blocks shortens it; shortening it would mean editing logic the rewrite does not need, which scope forbids. Nothing the rewrite leaves behind lengthened any other stage: the baseline's slowest stage (norm, hard multiply then one LUT, 6.03149 ns) is untouched and got faster only through placement.

Why no further revision: the only remaining soft logic in the converted region is the PE forwarding registers out_a/out_b and the per-PE down-cast, and neither can move inside a block — out_a and out_b are read by neighbouring PEs (4112-4143 and the rest of the array) and by nothing the block exposes, and the down-cast is not an arithmetic the block's ports carry. Feeding a block from in_a/in_b instead of out_a/out_b would place its result one cycle early and require 8 new soft flip-flops per PE to re-align, strictly worse on area than the current zero. Regrouping the 16 MACs of a row or column into one accumulating block is impossible: each PE's out_c is read separately (4453 / 4549 / 4646), so the 256 sums must stay 256 sums. Area, period and wirelength are therefore all at the floor of this rewrite family.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the norm and activation pipelines (5049-5105, 5465-5520) update their product registers under enables (enable_norm, in_data_available || norm_in_progress, validity_mask, enable_activation, activation_type); if those stages ran unconditionally with their inputs zeroed when idle, each product would become a single-term sum that an accumulating block cleared by the same reset could hold, putting 32 more products on blocks. Separately, bits [18:8] of the in_b/out_b chain (4443-4466 and the 19-bit b*to* wires at 4088-4103) feed nothing but further out_b registers and end unconnected (b_data_out is undriven at 4371 and tied to b_data_out_NC at 5877); narrowing that chain to 8 bits would make the dead-bit sweep explicit rather than relying on synthesis.
